Hand-maintained rather\nthan generated — these are questions, not outputs.\n\nOrdered roughly by how much the answer would change the picture.\n\n---\n\n## 1. The convergence in Køge Bugt\n\n**The claim.** Wind-driven southward surface transport meets the northward Baltic outflow,\nand the convergence accumulates fine sediment and floating material in Køge Bugt. The bay\nfunctions as a collecting basin for material mobilised elsewhere, not only for its own\ndischarge.\n\n**Why it matters.** Everything computed in `SEABED.md` is *local* resuspension from local\nwind. If a large share of the sediment and its contaminant load arrives from outside the\nbay, then local interventions cannot fix it, and the relevant catchment is far larger than\nthe one draining into it.\n\n**What would settle it.** Baltic Sea physics reanalysis — Copernicus Marine (CMEMS) has\ncurrent fields at adequate resolution, behind free registration. With hourly currents plus\nthe wind record already fetched, a particle-tracking run would show directly whether\nKøge Bugt is a convergence zone and where its material comes from.\n\n**Second-best.** Sediment provenance. An accumulation zone shows fine grain size, high\norganic content and elevated metals relative to its surroundings; fingerprinting could\ndistinguish locally-derived from imported material. Requires sediment samples.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Why the extremes did not respond to a halved load\n\nLand-based nitrogen supply is down roughly 50% since 1990. September oxygen-depletion\nextent in 2023 and 2024 was at or above the level of 1989, 2000 and 2002, when the load\nwas about double. Meanwhile 2025 came in at roughly a third of 2024's area. Neither the\nabsence of a trend nor the size of the year-to-year swing has an accepted explanation.\n\nThree candidates — legacy lag, warming, loss of assimilative state — are laid out in\n[CAUSATION.md](#CAUSATION.md). Lag fails on elapsed time. The other two have no term\nanywhere in a source apportionment, because neither is a source.\n\n**Why it matters.** This is the only empirical test of the nitrogen-dominant model that\nanyone has actually run, and it has been running for thirty-five years. If load is not\nthe dominant control on the extremes, then the apportionment is answering a question\nabout attribution while the policy is asking a question about outcomes.\n\n**What would settle it.** A regression of annual iltsvind extent on flow-normalised load,\nwind-work over the stratified season, and bottom-water temperature anomaly — with an\ninteraction term for state. All three series are published by DCE. No new data, no new\ninstruments. The regression appears not to have been published.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Whether \"ikke registreret\" means measured or unmeasured\n\nDCE reports state no oxygen depletion registered in Køge Bugt, including in 2023, the worst\niltsvind year in twenty. This document does the opposite: it grants the number and follows it *forward*, through the causal chain it is routinely quoted as describing.\n\n> **agriculture causes 70% of fedtemøg**\n\nThat sentence, or a close variant, is how the figure enters public argument. It is a claim about an outcome on a shore. The number behind it is a share of one term in one account. Between the two lie four links, and this document asks what coefficient each one has.\n\n## 1. What the number is a share of\n\nThe published 69.6% is agriculture's share of the **land-based waterborne term** — nitrogen arriving at the coast through Danish streams and unmonitored catchments. Those are two rows of the twenty enumerated in NITROGEN.md:\n\n| Row | kt N/yr | Status |\n|---|---:|---|\n| Danish land via monitored streams | 32 – 38 | Partly Measured |\n| Danish land via unmonitored catchments | 27 – 33 | Modelled |\n| **The term the percentage divides** | **59 – 71** | |\n| **69.6% of it — the agricultural load** | **41 – 49** | |\n\nSo the quantity in question is roughly **41–49 kt N/yr**. That is a real number and it is not small. The question is what it is a share *of*.\n\nThe enumerated pathways that carry any number at all sum to **167 – 327 kt N/yr**, with **10 of 20 rows empty**. An empty row can only add. So the denominator has a floor and no ceiling, and any share computed against it is a **ceiling, not an estimate**:\n\n> Agriculture accounts for **no more than 30%** of enumerated reactive nitrogen reaching Danish marine waters — and at the wide end of the denominator bounds, as little as 13%. Fill any of the empty rows and that ceiling falls. It cannot rise.\n\nThe published 69.6% and this ceiling are not competing estimates of the same thing. They answer different questions, and only the second one is the question the public sentence is asking.\n\nTwo omissions set the scale of the gap. **Atmospheric deposition to Danish marine waters is 45–65 kt N/yr** — comparable to the entire land-based term, falling on the water directly, and absent from every published apportionment. **Sediment regeneration** is not a source at all in the accounting frame, yet one study puts net advection at around a tenth of what annual primary production requires, which implies internal recycling supplies most of it.\n\n## 2. Four missing coefficients, reported as one number\n\nGranting the 69.6% entirely, here is what it has to survive to become a claim about fedtemøg:\n\n| Link | Coefficient | Status | Why |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Agriculture → the land-based waterborne nitrogen term | 69.6% | **PUBLISHED** | A residual: measured-plus-modelled total minus modelled point sources minus modelled natural background. Retention modelled at ±16 percentage points. Returns negative values in dry years. |\n| That term → all reactive nitrogen reaching the sea | no coefficient | **OPEN SET** | 10 of 20 enumerated pathways carry no number, including atmospheric deposition's organic fraction, submarine groundwater discharge, and sediment regeneration — probably the largest single supply to the productive layer. |\n| Nitrogen → oxygen depletion | no coefficient | **NOT COMPUTED** | Nitrogen is one of at least six oxygen sinks. There is no potency term: a kilogram in February into a mixed column counts the same as a kilogram in July under a pycnocline. No ventilation term, no state variable. |\n| Oxygen depletion → loss of higher life | no coefficient | **NOT COMPUTED** | Hypoxia is one route among several — toxicants, trawling, turbidity, sulphide exposure, physical loss of habitat. The soft-bottom survey runs 1 March–31 May, so the autumn kill is never observed, only its aftermath. |\n| Loss of higher life → fedtemøg on a shore | no coefficient | **NOT MEASURED AT ALL** | Fedtemøg has no national monitoring — not extent, not biomass, not duration. And it needs retention, stranding wind and a shore, which are properties of the receiving bay, not of the source. |\n\nEvery one of those links is a real causal connection. None of them has a number. The sentence multiplies them anyway, and the product inherits the appearance of the one factor that was measured.\n\nThis is not a claim that the links are weak. It is a claim about what kind of object the result is. **A product of four unknown fractions is an unknown fraction** — not a small one. Agriculture may still be the largest single contributor at every link. Nobody has computed it either way, and that is the finding.\n\nAnd section 3 argues the more serious objection: the chain is not a chain.\n\n## 3. Nitrogen is necessary for one pathway, not for the outcome\n\nSection 2 treats the argument as a chain and asks for each link's coefficient. That concedes too much, because it accepts the shape. The published framing is\n\n> nitrogen → primary production → biomass → the mush on the shore\n\nand that is one branch of a tree, not the trunk. There are at least three routes to the same endpoint, and **nitrogen is a necessary condition for exactly one of them**:\n\n| Route | What arrives | Does it need nitrogen? |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Growth** | dissolved nutrients | **Yes** — this is the modelled pathway |\n| **B. Direct organic matter** | organic carbon that is already biomass or was never alive at all — sewage solids, faecal matter, resuspended basin sludge, and fat | **No.** The material does not have to be grown. The flat 25% for oxygen depletion is discoverable because Timmermann, Christensen, Murray and Markager wrote 'det vurderes at en 25 % reduktion i TN-koncentrationen er minimumskrav for at ændre systemet'. The decision to average rather than maximise is discoverable because they explained that it minimises the risk of over-implementation. The exclusion of eelgrass is discoverable because they justified it by response time. The typetal's exclusions are discoverable because Miljøstyrelsen listed them. These are people documenting their own limitations in public. That is what good work looks like, and it is the only reason an audit is possible at all.\",\n \"the_capability_gap\": \"This audit was cheap in a way it could not have been when the work was done. The distance between what the document claims and what the politics claims is the finding. The document is the evidence for the finding, not the target of it.\",\n \"consequence_for_this_project\": \"Name methods, not people, wherever a name is not load-bearing. {"parentUuid":"1ebd3174-a9d0-43f0-b1f7-16e7f1cb4e5b","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a3d74de0ffc483c26","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Ceq8vsAN8xKoGyqomGqBs","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Shrinkage is the analogy — it's a real residual estimator that genuinely gets reported as \"theft\". Let me write it."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":769,"cache_read_input_tokens":959638,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":769,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null},"apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011Ceq8vj1yFXpw7XQVCdkb9","attributionAgent":"fork","type":"assistant","uuid":"cac7bc68-1ff0-401c-b0bd-e4ce20fcdb18","timestamp":"2026-09-08T01:53:56.434Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/user/projects/copenhagen-waterways","sessionId":"a357d1ab-60f3-479a-9941-a683a24b19ae","version":"2.1.263","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"harmonic-churning-simon"}
{"parentUuid":"cac7bc68-1ff0-401c-b0bd-e4ce20fcdb18","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a3d74de0ffc483c26","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Ceq8vsAN8xKoGyqomGqBs","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01TvqXevz4pTL5RrPvKv6wEF","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/user/projects/copenhagen-waterways/docs/RESIDUAL.md","content":"# The number that was never measured\n\nHand-written rather than generated. This page explains one idea, and it is the idea the\nrest of this site keeps running into. Nitrogen is the worked example. The idea is not\nabout nitrogen, and you do not need to care about Denmark to use it.\n\n---\n\n## A shop at closing time\n\nA supermarket wants to know how much stock it lost to shoplifting last year.\n\nNobody can count shoplifting. By its nature it is the thing that happens when no one is\nrecording. So the shop does the only thing available: it works out what *should* be on the\nshelves from its delivery records and its till records, counts what is *actually* there,\nand calls the gap **shrinkage**.\n\nShrinkage is a real, useful, carefully produced number. Every large retailer computes it.\nAnd then, reliably, it appears in the newspaper as *\"shoplifting cost retailers £2.2bn\"*.\n\nLook at what happened in that sentence. Shrinkage is everything unaccounted for:\n\n- customer theft\n- staff theft\n- damaged goods thrown out and not recorded\n- spoilage\n- deliveries short of the invoice\n- items mispriced at the till\n- someone typing 12 instead of 21\n- and the plain error in both counts\n\nShoplifting is one item on that list. The number measures the list. The headline names\nthe item.\n\nThis page is about that move, why it is so easy to make, and how to notice it.\n\n---\n\n## What is actually going on\n\n### An estimator is not the thing it estimates\n\nThe thing you care about — how much was shoplifted — is called the **estimand**. It is out\nthere in the world and you cannot see it.\n\nWhat you can compute is an **estimator**: a recipe that takes measurements you *can* make\nand produces a number you hope is close. Shrinkage is an estimator. Shoplifting is the\nestimand.\n\nEverything in science works this way and there is nothing wrong with it. A thermometer is\nan estimator for temperature. The trouble starts when the estimator and the estimand swap\nnames, because then the assumptions that connected them stop travelling with the number.\n\n### A residual is a particular kind of estimator, and the most fragile kind\n\nSome estimators measure the thing directly, more or less. A residual estimator does the\nopposite. It measures *everything else* and keeps the leftovers:\n\n> **the thing I want = the total − all the parts I can account for**\n\nShrinkage is a residual. So is the Danish figure this site is about. So, as we will see,\nare several numbers you have read this month.\n\n### The errors add up. They do not cancel out\n\nThis is the part that surprises people, and it is the whole problem.\n\nSuppose the shop's delivery records are 1% out, its till records are 1% out, and its\nphysical count is 1% out. You might hope those wobbles partly cancel. They do not — not\nin the residual.\n\nThe residual is a *difference between large numbers*. If the shop turns over £50m and\nshrinkage is £1m, then a 1% error in the £50m figure is £500,000 — half the answer. The\nerrors in each input are small **relative to that input**, and enormous **relative to the\nleftovers**.\n\nSo a residual is always the noisiest quantity in the account. Not sometimes. Structurally,\nby construction, every time. It absorbs the uncertainty of everything it was computed\nfrom, and it has none of its own measurement to steady it.\n\n### And it can never be checked\n\nThis is the deeper trouble, and it is easy to miss.\n\nYou would like to validate shrinkage: measure shoplifting some other way, and see if the\nnumbers agree. **You cannot.** If you had an independent way to measure shoplifting, you\nwould not have needed the residual in the first place. The reason a quantity is computed\nas a leftover is precisely that it cannot be observed.\n\nSo a residual estimator is, by its own definition, **the one class of number that can\nnever be confirmed against the thing it claims to describe.** Every other number in the\naccount can be checked against something. This one cannot be checked against anything,\never, in principle.\n\nThat is not a criticism of anyone's work. It is a property of the arithmetic. It becomes a\ncriticism only when such a number is handed a legal obligation.\n\n### When it comes out impossible, that is the error bars talking\n\nSometimes a shop's stocktake finds *more* stock than the records allow. Negative\nshrinkage. Everyone shrugs and calls it a counting error, and moves on.\n\nThey should not move on, because that excursion is doing something useful: it is telling\nyou **how big the errors are**.\n\nA physical mass cannot be negative. So if your estimator produces a negative value, the\nsize of that negative number is a **lower bound on the error in your method** — not just\nin that year, but in every year. The years that come out looking sensible are not more\naccurate. They are the same method with the same noise, and the noise merely happened to\nland somewhere plausible.\n\nAn estimator that can return an impossible answer has published its own error bars, and\nalmost nobody reads them.\n\n---\n\n## The ladder\n\nHere is how a carefully qualified technical quantity becomes a claim about the world. Each\nstep is small. Each is individually defensible. Nobody along the way does anything they\nwould recognise as wrong.\n\n| | The sentence | What quietly dropped |\n|---|---|---|\n| **0** | *The residual, after subtracting the modelled parts from the measured total, is X.* | — this is what was actually computed |\n| **1** | *X is the shrinkage.* | the conditionality. X depends on every model that was subtracted, and those models are no longer mentioned |\n| **2** | *Shoplifting cost us X.* | **the estimator becomes the estimand.** The leftovers are renamed as one of their possible causes |\n| **3** | *Shoplifting is why our prices went up.* | a causal claim about an outcome, across a link nobody measured |\n| **4** | *We need more security guards.* | a policy, sized to a number that was never a measurement of the thing the policy targets |\n\nStep 2 is the load-bearing one, and it is the one that never gets argued, because it\nhappens in the choice of a word rather than in a claim. Nobody writes *\"we hereby assume\nthe residual is entirely shoplifting.\"* They just start calling it shoplifting.\n\nBy step 4 the number has a life of its own. Anyone who questions it is questioning\narithmetic that was done correctly — which it was.\n\n---\n\n## The worked example\n\nDenmark requires its farms to cut nitrogen. The figure everyone quotes is that\n**agriculture accounts for 69.6% of nitrogen**.\n\nThat figure is a residual. It is produced like this:\n\n> take the nitrogen measured and modelled arriving at the coast, subtract the modelled\n> contribution of sewage works and industry, subtract the modelled natural background,\n> and call what remains agriculture.\n\nEverything about it that follows is discoverable **because the people who built it wrote\nit down and published it**. That matters and this page returns to it at the end.\n\n**The inputs it inherits.** Of the land area involved, 49% is measured and 51% is\nmodelled. The measured half uses grab samples at intervals, and a study of three streams\nfound that method gave *lower* transport than continuous measurement **every time** — a\ndocumented one-directional bias. The natural background that gets subtracted is a model\noutput, and the retention term inside it carries an uncertainty of ±16 percentage points.\n\n**It fails the impossibility test.** In dry years — 1996, 2005 — the calculated\nagricultural contribution comes out **negative**. A mass of nitrogen cannot be less than\nnothing. That excursion is the method telling you the size of its own error, in every year.\n\n**It cannot be checked.** There is no independent measurement of \"nitrogen from\nagriculture\" to compare it against. If there were, nobody would compute it as a leftover.\n\n**And then it climbs the ladder.** *69.6% of the land-based waterborne term* becomes\n*agriculture causes 70% of the nitrogen*, becomes *70% of the oxygen depletion*, becomes\n*70% of the mess on the beach*. Two of those steps cross links where **no coefficient has\never been calculated** — the audit of that chain is in [CAUSATION.md](#CAUSATION.md).\n\nThere is a second detail worth knowing, because it shows the same instinct in a different\nplace. The oxygen-depletion requirement in the Danish method is not derived from any\nrelationship between nitrogen and oxygen. If a water body is flagged as oxygen-affected,\nthe method applies **a flat 25% cut**, chosen — in the method document's own words — as a\nfigure judged large enough to shift the system. It is a considered engineering default,\nhonestly labelled as one. It is not a measurement, and by the time it reaches a press\nrelease it is indistinguishable from one.\n\n---\n\n## You have read this number in other clothes\n\nThe pattern is not rare and it is not Danish. A short list, one line each, with no claim\nthat any of these is wrong:\n\n- **The output gap** — the difference between what an economy produces and what it\n  *could* produce. The second quantity is unobservable. The gap is a residual, and\n  interest rates are set with reference to it.\n- **NAIRU** — the unemployment rate below which inflation is said to accelerate. Not\n  measurable. Backed out of other quantities.\n- **Excess mortality** — actual deaths minus the deaths a model says you should have\n  expected. Sound and widely useful; also a difference between large numbers, and it\n  routinely goes negative.\n- **Attributable fraction** in epidemiology — the share of cases said to be caused by an\n  exposure. Computed from modelled risks, then reported as a count of people.\n- **Safety factors** in toxicology — the 10s and 100s dividing an observed dose to reach a\n  permitted one. Deliberate, disclosed, defensible engineering judgements. Also constants\n  someone chose, which then propagate through everything downstream as if measured.\n\nThe test is never *does this contain a judgement?* Every applied number contains\njudgements, and must. The test is three questions:\n\n1. **Is the judgement disclosed?**\n2. **Is the sensitivity to it reported** — what happens to the answer if it were 15%\n   instead of 25%?\n3. **Does the claim being made downstream respect it?**\n\nIn the nitrogen case the first is yes and the other two are no. And notice that only the\nfirst is the scientists'. The other two failures happen after the document leaves the\nbuilding.\n\n---\n\n## What a checkable number looks like instead\n\nThe opposite of a residual is not a better model. It is **two methods that fail\ndifferently, agreeing.**\n\nThis project can show one, from its own work. Copenhagen published a flood model as seven\nPDF maps with the geographical coordinates stripped out. Putting them back on the map\nproduced a number — where each sheet sits — and here is why that number can be believed:\n\n- Each sheet carries a **printed scale bar**. Separately, control points located by a\n  resident on a web map imply a scale of their own. Those two things have nothing in\n  common: one is ink on paper, the other is a person clicking. **They agree to 0.3%.**\n- The position implied by those human control points, and the position found by\n  **correlating the sheet's own painted water against real water**, land **within one\n  standard error** of each other. A tired person clicking and an FFT cross-correlation\n  fail in entirely unrelated ways.\n- Some of the model's painted flood depth falls on open water, which is an error by\n  construction — water standing on water. After the alignment, that error fell by **22%**.\n  Nothing in the alignment was optimising for it. It was measured against the city's water\n  polygons, which the alignment never reads.\n\nThree checks, three unrelated failure modes, all agreeing. That is what makes a number\nload-bearing — not the authority of whoever produced it, and not the sophistication of the\nmethod.\n\nA residual estimator can never have this. That is the argument of this page in one line.\n\n---\n\n## The same standard, turned around\n\nIt would be dishonest to describe this test and not apply it here. The following claims on\nthis site currently have **no independent check**, and should be read accordingly:\n\n- **The ceiling of 30%** on agriculture's share of nitrogen reaching the sea. It depends\n  entirely on our own enumeration of twenty pathways being right, and on our own bounds\n  for the ten that carry numbers. Nobody has checked the enumeration.\n- **The wave and bed-shear model.** Computed exceedance frequencies against literature\n  values for critical shear stress. No observation of sediment actually moving. The\n  live-bed versus dead-bed *ratio* is robust; the absolute hours are not.\n- **The 73-day retention figure for Køge Bugt.** One coarse global model. And the same\n  method put Det Sydfynske Øhav mid-range, which was against expectation — a result we\n  reported rather than explained.\n- **The 12-hour transport lag** between overflow-scale rain and southward flow. 145\n  event-hours, six lags tested, one came out strong. A longer record would settle it.\n- **The Nørrebro flood sheet.** Every attempt to correlate it against a neighbour came out\n  flat. It has no control points either. It is the one sheet not known to be consistent\n  with the other six, and it is marked as such.\n- **The extractive-aquaculture area estimate.** A stoichiometric calculation. Never\n  compared against what a working mussel farm actually removes.\n- **The seasonal argument about fedtemøg.** It rests on wind records and on testimony.\n  There is no monitoring of the shore condition anywhere in Denmark, which is the finding\n  — and it also means our own seasonal claim cannot presently be checked either.\n\nSeveral of those could be checked cheaply. That they have not been is a fact about this\nproject, not a defence of it.\n\n---\n\n## A note on who this is about\n\nEvery limitation described here was found by **reading a document that its authors wrote\nand published**. The 25% is discoverable because the method document states it and\nexplains the reasoning. The averaging of indicators is discoverable because they wrote\ndown that it reduces the risk of over-implementation. The exclusion of eelgrass is\ndiscoverable because they justified it, in print, by its slow response time. The sampling\nexclusions in the toxicant figures are discoverable because the agency listed them.\n\nThat is people documenting their own limitations in public. It is what good work looks\nlike, and it is the only reason any of this could be audited at all.\n\nIt is also worth saying that this audit was cheap in a way it could not have been when the\nwork was done. Reading a methodology document against thirty-one years of hourly wind\nrecords, a recovered flood model and four separate government reports — and holding all of\nit at once — was months of specialist labour a decade ago. Holding past work to a standard\nthat did not exist when it was done is anachronism, not criticism.\n\n**The criticism here is of the use of a number, not its production.** A method that states\nin its own text that it averages indicators to avoid over-implementation, that the oxygen\nrequirement is a judged constant, and that most water bodies have a single monitoring\nstation, becomes *\"agriculture causes 70% of the mess on the beach\"* only after it leaves\nthe scientists' hands.\n\nThat distance — between what the document says and what the politics says — is the\nfinding. The document is the evidence for it, not the target.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":769,"cache_read_input_tokens":959638,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":769,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null},"apiBlockIndex":2,"requestId":"req_011Ceq8vj1yFXpw7XQVCdkb9","attributionAgent":"fork","type":"assistant","uuid":"95619ff8-b481-4795-a2fb-bac210f16e29","timestamp":"2026-09-08T01:55:12.574Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/user/projects/copenhagen-waterways","sessionId":"a357d1ab-60f3-479a-9941-a683a24b19ae","version":"2.1.263","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"harmonic-churning-simon"}
{"parentUuid":"95619ff8-b481-4795-a2fb-bac210f16e29","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8466ba53-c2d3-48d8-830a-8d25f83bea98","agentId":"a3d74de0ffc483c26","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TvqXevz4pTL5RrPvKv6wEF","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /home/user/projects/copenhagen-waterways/docs/RESIDUAL.md (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)"}]},"uuid":"34e5e0cb-22e1-47f2-8283-e21be71c678f","timestamp":"2026-09-08T01:55:12.624Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"create","filePath":"/home/user/projects/copenhagen-waterways/docs/RESIDUAL.md","content":"# The number that was never measured\n\nHand-written rather than generated. This page explains one idea, and it is the idea the\nrest of this site keeps running into. Nitrogen is the worked example. The idea is not\nabout nitrogen, and you do not need to care about Denmark to use it.\n\n---\n\n## A shop at closing time\n\nA supermarket wants to know how much stock it lost to shoplifting last year.\n\nNobody can count shoplifting. By its nature it is the thing that happens when no one is\nrecording. So the shop does the only thing available: it works out what *should* be on the\nshelves from its delivery records and its till records, counts what is *actually* there,\nand calls the gap **shrinkage**.\n\nShrinkage is a real, useful, carefully produced number. Every large retailer computes it.\nAnd then, reliably, it appears in the newspaper as *\"shoplifting cost retailers £2.2bn\"*.\n\nLook at what happened in that sentence. Shrinkage is everything unaccounted for:\n\n- customer theft\n- staff theft\n- damaged goods thrown out and not recorded\n- spoilage\n- deliveries short of the invoice\n- items mispriced at the till\n- someone typing 12 instead of 21\n- and the plain error in both counts\n\nShoplifting is one item on that list. The number measures the list. The headline names\nthe item.\n\nThis page is about that move, why it is so easy to make, and how to notice it.\n\n---\n\n## What is actually going on\n\n### An estimator is not the thing it estimates\n\nThe thing you care about — how much was shoplifted — is called the **estimand**. It is out\nthere in the world and you cannot see it.\n\nWhat you can compute is an **estimator**: a recipe that takes measurements you *can* make\nand produces a number you hope is close. Shrinkage is an estimator. Shoplifting is the\nestimand.\n\nEverything in science works this way and there is nothing wrong with it. A thermometer is\nan estimator for temperature. The trouble starts when the estimator and the estimand swap\nnames, because then the assumptions that connected them stop travelling with the number.\n\n### A residual is a particular kind of estimator, and the most fragile kind\n\nSome estimators measure the thing directly, more or less. A residual estimator does the\nopposite. It measures *everything else* and keeps the leftovers:\n\n> **the thing I want = the total − all the parts I can account for**\n\nShrinkage is a residual. So is the Danish figure this site is about. So, as we will see,\nare several numbers you have read this month.\n\n### The errors add up. They do not cancel out\n\nThis is the part that surprises people, and it is the whole problem.\n\nSuppose the shop's delivery records are 1% out, its till records are 1% out, and its\nphysical count is 1% out. You might hope those wobbles partly cancel. They do not — not\nin the residual.\n\nThe residual is a *difference between large numbers*. If the shop turns over £50m and\nshrinkage is £1m, then a 1% error in the £50m figure is £500,000 — half the answer. The\nerrors in each input are small **relative to that input**, and enormous **relative to the\nleftovers**.\n\nSo a residual is always the noisiest quantity in the account. Not sometimes. Structurally,\nby construction, every time. It absorbs the uncertainty of everything it was computed\nfrom, and it has none of its own measurement to steady it.\n\n### And it can never be checked\n\nThis is the deeper trouble, and it is easy to miss.\n\nYou would like to validate shrinkage: measure shoplifting some other way, and see if the\nnumbers agree. **You cannot.** If you had an independent way to measure shoplifting, you\nwould not have needed the residual in the first place. The reason a quantity is computed\nas a leftover is precisely that it cannot be observed.\n\nSo a residual estimator is, by its own definition, **the one class of number that can\nnever be confirmed against the thing it claims to describe.** Every other number in the\naccount can be checked against something. This one cannot be checked against anything,\never, in principle.\n\nThat is not a criticism of anyone's work. It is a property of the arithmetic. It becomes a\ncriticism only when such a number is handed a legal obligation.\n\n### When it comes out impossible, that is the error bars talking\n\nSometimes a shop's stocktake finds *more* stock than the records allow. Negative\nshrinkage. Everyone shrugs and calls it a counting error, and moves on.\n\nThey should not move on, because that excursion is doing something useful: it is telling\nyou **how big the errors are**.\n\nA physical mass cannot be negative. So if your estimator produces a negative value, the\nsize of that negative number is a **lower bound on the error in your method** — not just\nin that year, but in every year. The years that come out looking sensible are not more\naccurate. They are the same method with the same noise, and the noise merely happened to\nland somewhere plausible.\n\nAn estimator that can return an impossible answer has published its own error bars, and\nalmost nobody reads them.\n\n---\n\n## The ladder\n\nHere is how a carefully qualified technical quantity becomes a claim about the world. Each\nstep is small. Each is individually defensible. Nobody along the way does anything they\nwould recognise as wrong.\n\n| | The sentence | What quietly dropped |\n|---|---|---|\n| **0** | *The residual, after subtracting the modelled parts from the measured total, is X.* | — this is what was actually computed |\n| **1** | *X is the shrinkage.* | the conditionality. X depends on every model that was subtracted, and those models are no longer mentioned |\n| **2** | *Shoplifting cost us X.* | **the estimator becomes the estimand.** The leftovers are renamed as one of their possible causes |\n| **3** | *Shoplifting is why our prices went up.* | a causal claim about an outcome, across a link nobody measured |\n| **4** | *We need more security guards.* | a policy, sized to a number that was never a measurement of the thing the policy targets |\n\nStep 2 is the load-bearing one, and it is the one that never gets argued, because it\nhappens in the choice of a word rather than in a claim. Nobody writes *\"we hereby assume\nthe residual is entirely shoplifting.\"* They just start calling it shoplifting.\n\nBy step 4 the number has a life of its own. Anyone who questions it is questioning\narithmetic that was done correctly — which it was.\n\n---\n\n## The worked example\n\nDenmark requires its farms to cut nitrogen. {"parentUuid":"898d7c17-342e-4ddb-a4ff-c5fb23d8af76","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a3d74de0ffc483c26","attachment":{"type":"file","filename":"/home/user/projects/copenhagen-waterways/docs/RESIDUAL.md","content":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/user/projects/copenhagen-waterways/docs/RESIDUAL.md","content":"# The number that was never measured\n\nHand-written rather than generated. This page explains one idea, and it is the idea the\nrest of this site keeps running into. Nitrogen is the worked example. The idea is not\nabout nitrogen, and you do not need to care about Denmark to use it.\n\n---\n\n## A shop at closing time\n\nA supermarket wants to know how much stock it lost to shoplifting last year.\n\nNobody can count shoplifting. By its nature it is the thing that happens when no one is\nrecording. So the shop does the only thing available: it works out what *should* be on the\nshelves from its delivery records and its till records, counts what is *actually* there,\nand calls the gap **shrinkage**.\n\nShrinkage is a real, useful, carefully produced number. Every large retailer computes it.\nAnd then, reliably, it appears in the newspaper as *\"shoplifting cost retailers £2.2bn\"*.\n\nLook at what happened in that sentence. Shrinkage is everything unaccounted for:\n\n- customer theft\n- staff theft\n- damaged goods thrown out and not recorded\n- spoilage\n- deliveries short of the invoice\n- items mispriced at the till\n- someone typing 12 instead of 21\n- and the plain error in both counts\n\nShoplifting is one item on that list. The number measures the list. The headline names\nthe item.\n\nThis page is about that move, why it is so easy to make, and how to notice it.\n\n---\n\n## What is actually going on\n\n### An estimator is not the thing it estimates\n\nThe thing you care about — how much was shoplifted — is called the **estimand**. It is out\nthere in the world and you cannot see it.\n\nWhat you can compute is an **estimator**: a recipe that takes measurements you *can* make\nand produces a number you hope is close. Shrinkage is an estimator. Shoplifting is the\nestimand.\n\nEverything in science works this way and there is nothing wrong with it. A thermometer is\nan estimator for temperature. The trouble starts when the estimator and the estimand swap\nnames, because then the assumptions that connected them stop travelling with the number.\n\n### A residual is a particular kind of estimator, and the most fragile kind\n\nSome estimators measure the thing directly, more or less. A residual estimator does the\nopposite. It measures *everything else* and keeps the leftovers:\n\n> **the thing I want = the total − all the parts I can account for**\n\nShrinkage is a residual. So is the Danish figure this site is about. So, as we will see,\nare several numbers you have read this month.\n\n### The errors add up. They do not cancel out\n\nThis is the part that surprises people, and it is the whole problem.\n\nSuppose the shop's delivery records are 1% out, its till records are 1% out, and its\nphysical count is 1% out. You might hope those wobbles partly cancel. They do not — not\nin the residual.\n\nThe residual is a *difference between large numbers*. If the shop turns over £50m and\nshrinkage is £1m, then a 1% error in the £50m figure is £500,000 — half the answer. The\nerrors in each input are small **relative to that input**, and enormous **relative to the\nleftovers**.\n\nSo a residual is always the noisiest quantity in the account. Not sometimes. Structurally,\nby construction, every time. It absorbs the uncertainty of everything it was computed\nfrom, and it has none of its own measurement to steady it.\n\n### And it can never be checked\n\nThis is the deeper trouble, and it is easy to miss.\n\nYou would like to validate shrinkage: measure shoplifting some other way, and see if the\nnumbers agree. **You cannot.** If you had an independent way to measure shoplifting, you\nwould not have needed the residual in the first place. The reason a quantity is computed\nas a leftover is precisely that it cannot be observed.\n\nSo a residual estimator is, by its own definition, **the one class of number that can\nnever be confirmed against the thing it claims to describe.** Every other number in the\naccount can be checked against something. This one cannot be checked against anything,\never, in principle.\n\nThat is not a criticism of anyone's work. It is a property of the arithmetic. It becomes a\ncriticism only when such a number is handed a legal obligation.\n\n### When it comes out impossible, that is the error bars talking\n\nSometimes a shop's stocktake finds *more* stock than the records allow. Negative\nshrinkage. Everyone shrugs and calls it a counting error, and moves on.\n\nThey should not move on, because that excursion is doing something useful: it is telling\nyou **how big the errors are**.\n\nA physical mass cannot be negative. So if your estimator produces a negative value, the\nsize of that negative number is a **lower bound on the error in your method** — not just\nin that year, but in every year. The years that come out looking sensible are not more\naccurate. They are the same method with the same noise, and the noise merely happened to\nland somewhere plausible.\n\nAn estimator that can return an impossible answer has published its own error bars, and\nalmost nobody reads them.\n\n---\n\n## The ladder\n\nHere is how a carefully qualified technical quantity becomes a claim about the world. Each\nstep is small. Each is individually defensible. Nobody along the way does anything they\nwould recognise as wrong.\n\n| | The sentence | What quietly dropped |\n|---|---|---|\n| **0** | *The residual, after subtracting the modelled parts from the measured total, is X.* | — this is what was actually computed |\n| **1** | *X is the shrinkage.* | the conditionality. X depends on every model that was subtracted, and those models are no longer mentioned |\n| **2** | *Shoplifting cost us X.* | **the estimator becomes the estimand.** The leftovers are renamed as one of their possible causes |\n| **3** | *Shoplifting is why our prices went up.* | a causal claim about an outcome, across a link nobody measured |\n| **4** | *We need more security guards.* | a policy, sized to a number that was never a measurement of the thing the policy targets |\n\nStep 2 is the load-bearing one, and it is the one that never gets argued, because it\nhappens in the choice of a word rather than in a claim. Nobody writes *\"we hereby assume\nthe residual is entirely shoplifting.\"* They just start calling it shoplifting.\n\nBy step 4 the number has a life of its own. Anyone who questions it is questioning\narithmetic that was done correctly — which it was.\n\n---\n\n## The worked example\n\nDenmark requires its farms to cut nitrogen. The figure everyone quotes is that\n**agriculture accounts for 69.6% of nitrogen**.\n\nThat figure is a residual. It is produced like this:\n\n> take the nitrogen measured and modelled arriving at the coast, subtract the modelled\n> contribution of sewage works and industry, subtract the modelled natural background,\n> and call what remains agriculture.\n\nEverything about it that follows is discoverable **because the people who built it wrote\nit down and published it**. That matters and this page returns to it at the end.\n\n**The inputs it inherits.** Of the land area involved, 49% is measured and 51% is\nmodelled. The measured half uses grab samples at intervals, and a study of three streams\nfound that method gave *lower* transport than continuous measurement **every time** — a\ndocumented one-directional bias. The natural background that gets subtracted is a model\noutput, and the retention term inside it carries an uncertainty of ±16 percentage points.\n\n**It fails the impossibility test.** In dry years — 1996, 2005 — the calculated\nagricultural contribution comes out **negative**. A mass of nitrogen cannot be less than\nnothing. That excursion is the method telling you the size of its own error, in every year.\n\n**It cannot be checked.** There is no independent measurement of \"nitrogen from\nagriculture\" to compare it against. If there were, nobody would compute it as a leftover.\n\n**And then it climbs the ladder.** *69.6% of the land-based waterborne term* becomes\n*agriculture causes 70% of the nitrogen*, becomes *70% of the oxygen depletion*, becomes\n*70% of the mess on the beach*. Two of those steps cross links where **no coefficient has\never been calculated** — the audit of that chain is in [CAUSATION.md](#CAUSATION.md).\n\nThere is a second detail worth knowing, because it shows the same instinct in a different\nplace. The oxygen-depletion requirement in the Danish method is not derived from any\nrelationship between nitrogen and oxygen. If a water body is flagged as oxygen-affected,\nthe method applies **a flat 25% cut**, chosen — in the method document's own words — as a\nfigure judged large enough to shift the system. It is a considered engineering default,\nhonestly labelled as one. It is not a measurement, and by the time it reaches a press\nrelease it is indistinguishable from one.\n\n---\n\n## You have read this number in other clothes\n\nThe pattern is not rare and it is not Danish. A short list, one line each, with no claim\nthat any of these is wrong:\n\n- **The output gap** — the difference between what an economy produces and what it\n  *could* produce. The second quantity is unobservable. The gap is a residual, and\n  interest rates are set with reference to it.\n- **NAIRU** — the unemployment rate below which inflation is said to accelerate. Not\n  measurable. Backed out of other quantities.\n- **Excess mortality** — actual deaths minus the deaths a model says you should have\n  expected. Sound and widely useful; also a difference between large numbers, and it\n  routinely goes negative.\n- **Attributable fraction** in epidemiology — the share of cases said to be caused by an\n  exposure. Computed from modelled risks, then reported as a count of people.\n- **Safety factors** in toxicology — the 10s and 100s dividing an observed dose to reach a\n  permitted one. Deliberate, disclosed, defensible engineering judgements. Also constants\n  someone chose, which then propagate through everything downstream as if measured.\n\nThe test is never *does this contain a judgement?* Every applied number contains\njudgements, and must. The test is three questions:\n\n1. **Is the judgement disclosed?**\n2. **Is the sensitivity to it reported** — what happens to the answer if it were 15%\n   instead of 25%?\n3. **Does the claim being made downstream respect it?**\n\nIn the nitrogen case the first is yes and the other two are no. And notice that only the\nfirst is the scientists'. The other two failures happen after the document leaves the\nbuilding.\n\n---\n\n## What a checkable number looks like instead\n\nThe opposite of a residual is not a better model. It is **two methods that fail\ndifferently, agreeing.**\n\nThis project can show one, from its own work. Copenhagen published a flood model as seven\nPDF maps with the geographical coordinates stripped out. Putting them back on the map\nproduced a number — where each sheet sits — and here is why that number can be believed:\n\n- Each sheet carries a **printed scale bar**. Separately, control points located by a\n  resident on a web map imply a scale of their own. Those two things have nothing in\n  common: one is ink on paper, the other is a person clicking. **They agree to 0.3%.**\n- The position implied by those human control points, and the position found by\n  **correlating the sheet's own painted water against real water**, land **within one\n  standard error** of each other. A tired person clicking and an FFT cross-correlation\n  fail in entirely unrelated ways.\n- Some of the model's painted flood depth falls on open water, which is an error by\n  construction — water standing on water. After the alignment, that error fell by **22%**.\n  Nothing in the alignment was optimising for it. It was measured against the city's water\n  polygons, which the alignment never reads.\n\nThree checks, three unrelated failure modes, all agreeing. That is what makes a number\nload-bearing — not the authority of whoever produced it, and not the sophistication of the\nmethod.\n\nA residual estimator can never have this. That is the argument of this page in one line.\n\n---\n\n## The same standard, turned around\n\nIt would be dishonest to describe this test and not apply it here. The following claims on\nthis site currently have **no independent check**, and should be read accordingly:\n\n- **The ceiling of 30%** on agriculture's share of nitrogen reaching the sea. It depends\n  entirely on our own enumeration of twenty pathways being right, and on our own bounds\n  for the ten that carry numbers. Nobody has checked the enumeration.\n- **The wave and bed-shear model.** Computed exceedance frequencies against literature\n  values for critical shear stress. No observation of sediment actually moving. The\n  live-bed versus dead-bed *ratio* is robust; the absolute hours are not.\n- **The 73-day retention figure for Køge Bugt.** One coarse global model. And the same\n  method put Det Sydfynske Øhav mid-range, which was against expectation — a result we\n  reported rather than explained.\n- **The 12-hour transport lag** between overflow-scale rain and southward flow. 145\n  event-hours, six lags tested, one came out strong. A longer record would settle it.\n- **The Nørrebro flood sheet.** Every attempt to correlate it against a neighbour came out\n  flat. It has no control points either. It is the one sheet not known to be consistent\n  with the other six, and it is marked as such.\n- **The extractive-aquaculture area estimate.** A stoichiometric calculation. Never\n  compared against what a working mussel farm actually removes.\n- **The seasonal argument about fedtemøg.** It rests on wind records and on testimony.\n  There is no monitoring of the shore condition anywhere in Denmark, which is the finding\n  — and it also means our own seasonal claim cannot presently be checked either.\n\nSeveral of those could be checked cheaply. That they have not been is a fact about this\nproject, not a defence of it.\n\n---\n\n## A note on who this is about\n\nEvery limitation described here was found by **reading a document that its authors wrote\nand published**. The 25% is discoverable because the method document states it and\nexplains the reasoning. The averaging of indicators is discoverable because they wrote\ndown that it reduces the risk of over-implementation. The exclusion of eelgrass is\ndiscoverable because they justified it, in print, by its slow response time. The sampling\nexclusions in the toxicant figures are discoverable because the agency listed them.\n\nThat is people documenting their own limitations in public. It is what good work looks\nlike, and it is the only reason any of this could be audited at all.\n\nIt is also worth saying that this audit was cheap in a way it could not have been when the\nwork was done. Reading a methodology document against thirty-one years of hourly wind\nrecords, a recovered flood model and four separate government reports — and holding all of\nit at once — was months of specialist labour a decade ago. Holding past work to a standard\nthat did not exist when it was done is anachronism, not criticism.\n\n**The criticism here is of the use of a number, not its production.** A method that states\nin its own text that it averages indicators to avoid over-implementation, that the oxygen\nrequirement is a judged constant, and that most water bodies have a single monitoring\nstation, becomes *\"agriculture causes 70% of the mess on the beach\"* only after it leaves\nthe scientists' hands.\n\nThat distance — between what the document says and what the politics says — is the\nfinding. The document is the evidence for it, not the target.\n","numLines":300,"startLine":1,"totalLines":300}},"displayPath":"docs/RESIDUAL.md"},"type":"attachment","uuid":"0c62b8cc-94f6-4c4c-b15d-7aaf75c18cca","timestamp":"2026-09-08T01:56:52.251Z","rendered":[{"content":"<system-reminder>\nCalled the Read tool with the following input: {\"file_path\":\"/home/user/projects/copenhagen-waterways/docs/RESIDUAL.md\"}\n</system-reminder>"},{"content":"<system-reminder>\nResult of calling the Read tool:\n1\t# The number that was never measured\n2\t\n3\tHand-written rather than generated. This page explains one idea, and it is the idea the\n4\trest of this site keeps running into. Nitrogen is the worked example. The idea is not\n5\tabout nitrogen, and you do not need to care about Denmark to use it.\n6\t\n7\t---\n8\t\n9\t## A shop at closing time\n10\t\n11\tA supermarket wants to know how much stock it lost to shoplifting last year.\n12\t\n13\tNobody can count shoplifting. By its nature it is the thing that happens when no one is\n14\trecording. So the shop does the only thing available: it works out what *should* be on the\n15\tshelves from its delivery records and its till records, counts what is *actually* there,\n16\tand calls the gap **shrinkage**.\n17\t\n18\tShrinkage is a real, useful, carefully produced number. Every large retailer computes it.\n19\tAnd then, reliably, it appears in the newspaper as *\"shoplifting cost retailers £2.2bn\"*.\n20\t\n21\tLook at what happened in that sentence. Shrinkage is everything unaccounted for:\n22\t\n23\t- customer theft\n24\t- staff theft\n25\t- damaged goods thrown out and not recorded\n26\t- spoilage\n27\t- deliveries short of the invoice\n28\t- items mispriced at the till\n29\t- someone typing 12 instead of 21\n30\t- and the plain error in both counts\n31\t\n32\tShoplifting is one item on that list. The number measures the list. The headline names\n33\tthe item.\n34\t\n35\tThis page is about that move, why it is so easy to make, and how to notice it.\n36\t\n37\t---\n38\t\n39\t## What is actually going on\n40\t\n41\t### An estimator is not the thing it estimates\n42\t\n43\tThe thing you care about — how much was shoplifted — is called the **estimand**. It is out\n44\tthere in the world and you cannot see it.\n45\t\n46\tWhat you can compute is an **estimator**: a recipe that takes measurements you *can* make\n47\tand produces a number you hope is close. Shrinkage is an estimator. Shoplifting is the\n48\testimand.\n49\t\n50\tEverything in science works this way and there is nothing wrong with it. A thermometer is\n51\tan estimator for temperature. The trouble starts when the estimator and the estimand swap\n52\tnames, because then the assumptions that connected them stop travelling with the number.\n53\t\n54\t### A residual is a particular kind of estimator, and the most fragile kind\n55\t\n56\tSome estimators measure the thing directly, more or less. A residual estimator does the\n57\topposite. It measures *everything else* and keeps the leftovers:\n58\t\n59\t> **the thing I want = the total − all the parts I can account for**\n60\t\n61\tShrinkage is a residual. So is the Danish figure this site is about. So, as we will see,\n62\tare several numbers you have read this month.\n63\t\n64\t### The errors add up. They do not cancel out\n65\t\n66\tThis is the part that surprises people, and it is the whole problem.\n67\t\n68\tSuppose the shop's delivery records are 1% out, its till records are 1% out, and its\n69\tphysical count is 1% out. You might hope those wobbles partly cancel. They do not — not\n70\tin the residual.\n71\t\n72\tThe residual is a *difference between large numbers*. If the shop turns over £50m and\n73\tshrinkage is £1m, then a 1% error in the £50m figure is £500,000 — half the answer. The\n74\terrors in each input are small **relative to that input**, and enormous **relative to the\n75\tleftovers**.\n76\t\n77\tSo a residual is always the noisiest quantity in the account. Not sometimes. Structurally,\n78\tby construction, every time. It absorbs the uncertainty of everything it was computed\n79\tfrom, and it has none of its own measurement to steady it.\n80\t\n81\t### And it can never be checked\n82\t\n83\tThis is the deeper trouble, and it is easy to miss.\n84\t\n85\tYou would like to validate shrinkage: measure shoplifting some other way, and see if the\n86\tnumbers agree. **You cannot.** If you had an independent way to measure shoplifting, you\n87\twould not have needed the residual in the first place. The reason a quantity is computed\n88\tas a leftover is precisely that it cannot be observed.\n89\t\n90\tSo a residual estimator is, by its own definition, **the one class of number that can\n91\tnever be confirmed against the thing it claims to describe.** Every other number in the\n92\taccount can be checked against something. This one cannot be checked against anything,\n93\tever, in principle.\n94\t\n95\tThat is not a criticism of anyone's work. It is a property of the arithmetic. It becomes a\n96\tcriticism only when such a number is handed a legal obligation.\n97\t\n98\t### When it comes out impossible, that is the error bars talking\n99\t\n100\tSometimes a shop's stocktake finds *more* stock than the records allow. Negative\n101\tshrinkage. Everyone shrugs and calls it a counting error, and moves on.\n102\t\n103\tThey should not move on, because that excursion is doing something useful: it is telling\n104\tyou **how big the errors are**.\n105\t\n106\tA physical mass cannot be negative. So if your estimator produces a negative value, the\n107\tsize of that negative number is a **lower bound on the error in your method** — not just\n108\tin that year, but in every year. The years that come out looking sensible are not more\n109\taccurate. They are the same method with the same noise, and the noise merely happened to\n110\tland somewhere plausible.\n111\t\n112\tAn estimator that can return an impossible answer has published its own error bars, and\n113\talmost nobody reads them.\n114\t\n115\t---\n116\t\n117\t## The ladder\n118\t\n119\tHere is how a carefully qualified technical quantity becomes a claim about the world. Each\n120\tstep is small. Each is individually defensible. Nobody along the way does anything they\n121\twould recognise as wrong.\n122\t\n123\t| | The sentence | What quietly dropped |\n124\t|---|---|---|\n125\t| **0** | *The residual, after subtracting the modelled parts from the measured total, is X.* | — this is what was actually computed |\n126\t| **1** | *X is the shrinkage.* | the conditionality. X depends on every model that was subtracted, and those models are no longer mentioned |\n127\t| **2** | *Shoplifting cost us X.* | **the estimator becomes the estimand.** The leftovers are renamed as one of their possible causes |\n128\t| **3** | *Shoplifting is why our prices went up.* | a causal claim about an outcome, across a link nobody measured |\n129\t| **4** | *We need more security guards.* | a policy, sized to a number that was never a measurement of the thing the policy targets |\n130\t\n131\tStep 2 is the load-bearing one, and it is the one that never gets argued, because it\n132\thappens in the choice of a word rather than in a claim. Nobody writes *\"we hereby assume\n133\tthe residual is entirely shoplifting.\"* They just start calling it shoplifting.\n134\t\n135\tBy step 4 the number has a life of its own. Anyone who questions it is questioning\n136\tarithmetic that was done correctly — which it was.\n137\t\n138\t---\n139\t\n140\t## The worked example\n141\t\n142\tDenmark requires its farms to cut nitrogen. The figure everyone quotes is that\n143\t**agriculture accounts for 69.6% of nitrogen**.\n144\t\n145\tThat figure is a residual. It is produced like this:\n146\t\n147\t> take the nitrogen measured and modelled arriving at the coast, subtract the modelled\n148\t> contribution of sewage works and industry, subtract the modelled natural background,\n149\t> and call what remains agriculture.\n150\t\n151\tEverything about it that follows is discoverable **because the people who built it wrote\n152\tit down and published it**. That matters and this page returns to it at the end.\n153\t\n154\t**The inputs it inherits.** Of the land area involved, 49% is measured and 51% is\n155\tmodelled. The measured half uses grab samples at intervals, and a study of three streams\n156\tfound that method gave *lower* transport than continuous measurement **every time** — a\n157\tdocumented one-directional bias. The natural background that gets subtracted is a model\n158\toutput, and the retention term inside it carries an uncertainty of ±16 percentage points.\n159\t\n160\t**It fails the impossibility test.** In dry years — 1996, 2005 — the calculated\n161\tagricultural contribution comes out **negative**. A mass of nitrogen cannot be less than\n162\tnothing. That excursion is the method telling you the size of its own error, in every year.\n163\t\n164\t**It cannot be checked.** There is no independent measurement of \"nitrogen from\n165\tagriculture\" to compare it against. If there were, nobody would compute it as a leftover.\n166\t\n167\t**And then it climbs the ladder.** *69.6% of the land-based waterborne term* becomes\n168\t*agriculture causes 70% of the nitrogen*, becomes *70% of the oxygen depletion*, becomes\n169\t*70% of the mess on the beach*. Two of those steps cross links where **no coefficient has\n170\tever been calculated** — the audit of that chain is in [CAUSATION.md](#CAUSATION.md).\n171\t\n172\tThere is a second detail worth knowing, because it shows the same instinct in a different\n173\tplace. The oxygen-depletion requirement in the Danish method is not derived from any\n174\trelationship between nitrogen and oxygen. If a water body is flagged as oxygen-affected,\n175\tthe method applies **a flat 25% cut**, chosen — in the method document's own words — as a\n176\tfigure judged large enough to shift the system. It is a considered engineering default,\n177\thonestly labelled as one. It is not a measurement, and by the time it reaches a press\n178\trelease it is indistinguishable from one.\n179\t\n180\t---\n181\t\n182\t## You have read this number in other clothes\n183\t\n184\tThe pattern is not rare and it is not Danish. A short list, one line each, with no claim\n185\tthat any of these is wrong:\n186\t\n187\t- **The output gap** — the difference between what an economy produces and what it\n188\t  *could* produce. The second quantity is unobservable. The gap is a residual, and\n189\t  interest rates are set with reference to it.\n190\t- **NAIRU** — the unemployment rate below which inflation is said to accelerate. Not\n191\t  measurable. Backed out of other quantities.\n192\t- **Excess mortality** — actual deaths minus the deaths a model says you should have\n193\t  expected. Sound and widely useful; also a difference between large numbers, and it\n194\t  routinely goes negative.\n195\t- **Attributable fraction** in epidemiology — the share of cases said to be caused by an\n196\t  exposure. Computed from modelled risks, then reported as a count of people.\n197\t- **Safety factors** in toxicology — the 10s and 100s dividing an observed dose to reach a\n198\t  permitted one. Deliberate, disclosed, defensible engineering judgements. Also constants\n199\t  someone chose, which then propagate through everything downstream as if measured.\n200\t\n201\tThe test is never *does this contain a judgement?* Every applied number contains\n202\tjudgements, and must. The test is three questions:\n203\t\n204\t1. **Is the judgement disclosed?**\n205\t2. **Is the sensitivity to it reported** — what happens to the answer if it were 15%\n206\t   instead of 25%?\n207\t3. **Does the claim being made downstream respect it?**\n208\t\n209\tIn the nitrogen case the first is yes and the other two are no. And notice that only the\n210\tfirst is the scientists'. The other two failures happen after the document leaves the\n211\tbuilding.\n212\t\n213\t---\n214\t\n215\t## What a checkable number looks like instead\n216\t\n217\tThe opposite of a residual is not a better model. It is **two methods that fail\n218\tdifferently, agreeing.**\n219\t\n220\tThis project can show one, from its own work. Copenhagen published a flood model as seven\n221\tPDF maps with the geographical coordinates stripped out. Putting them back on the map\n222\tproduced a number — where each sheet sits — and here is why that number can be believed:\n223\t\n224\t- Each sheet carries a **printed scale bar**. Separately, control points located by a\n225\t  resident on a web map imply a scale of their own. Those two things have nothing in\n226\t  common: one is ink on paper, the other is a person clicking. **They agree to 0.3%.**\n227\t- The position implied by those human control points, and the position found by\n228\t  **correlating the sheet's own painted water against real water**, land **within one\n229\t  standard error** of each other. A tired person clicking and an FFT cross-correlation\n230\t  fail in entirely unrelated ways.\n231\t- Some of the model's painted flood depth falls on open water, which is an error by\n232\t  construction — water standing on water. After the alignment, that error fell by **22%**.\n233\t  Nothing in the alignment was optimising for it. It was measured against the city's water\n234\t  polygons, which the alignment never reads.\n235\t\n236\tThree checks, three unrelated failure modes, all agreeing. That is what makes a number\n237\tload-bearing — not the authority of whoever produced it, and not the sophistication of the\n238\tmethod.\n239\t\n240\tA residual estimator can never have this. That is the argument of this page in one line.\n241\t\n242\t---\n243\t\n244\t## The same standard, turned around\n245\t\n246\tIt would be dishonest to describe this test and not apply it here. The following claims on\n247\tthis site currently have **no independent check**, and should be read accordingly:\n248\t\n249\t- **The ceiling of 30%** on agriculture's share of nitrogen reaching the sea. It depends\n250\t  entirely on our own enumeration of twenty pathways being right, and on our own bounds\n251\t  for the ten that carry numbers. Nobody has checked the enumeration.\n252\t- **The wave and bed-shear model.** Computed exceedance frequencies against literature\n253\t  values for critical shear stress. No observation of sediment actually moving. The\n254\t  live-bed versus dead-bed *ratio* is robust; the absolute hours are not.\n255\t- **The 73-day retention figure for Køge Bugt.** One coarse global model. And the same\n256\t  method put Det Sydfynske Øhav mid-range, which was against expectation — a result we\n257\t  reported rather than explained.\n258\t- **The 12-hour transport lag** between overflow-scale rain and southward flow. 145\n259\t  event-hours, six lags tested, one came out strong. A longer record would settle it.\n260\t- **The Nørrebro flood sheet.** Every attempt to correlate it against a neighbour came out\n261\t  flat. It has no control points either. It is the one sheet not known to be consistent\n262\t  with the other six, and it is marked as such.\n263\t- **The extractive-aquaculture area estimate.** A stoichiometric calculation. Never\n264\t  compared against what a working mussel farm actually removes.\n265\t- **The seasonal argument about fedtemøg.** It rests on wind records and on testimony.\n266\t  There is no monitoring of the shore condition anywhere in Denmark, which is the finding\n267\t  — and it also means our own seasonal claim cannot presently be checked either.\n268\t\n269\tSeveral of those could be checked cheaply. That they have not been is a fact about this\n270\tproject, not a defence of it.\n271\t\n272\t---\n273\t\n274\t## A note on who this is about\n275\t\n276\tEvery limitation described here was found by **reading a document that its authors wrote\n277\tand published**. The averaging of indicators is discoverable because they wrote\n279\tdown that it reduces the risk of over-implementation. The exclusion of eelgrass is\n280\tdiscoverable because they justified it, in print, by its slow response time. It is what good work looks\n284\tlike, and it is the only reason any of this could be audited at all.\n285\t\n286\tIt is also worth saying that this audit was cheap in a way it could not have been when the\n287\twork was done. Reading a methodology document against thirty-one years of hourly wind\n288\trecords, a recovered flood model and four separate government reports — and holding all of\n289\tit at once — was months of specialist labour a decade ago. Holding past work to a standard\n290\tthat did not exist when it was done is anachronism, not criticism.\n291\t\n292\t**The criticism here is of the use of a number, not its production.** A method that states\n293\tin its own text that it averages indicators to avoid over-implementation, that the oxygen\n294\trequirement is a judged constant, and that most water bodies have a single monitoring\n295\tstation, becomes *\"agriculture causes 70% of the mess on the beach\"* only after it leaves\n296\tthe scientists' hands.\n297\t\n298\tThat distance — between what the document says and what the politics says — is the\n299\tfinding. This project put it\nback: depth bands read from the palette, scale from each sheet's own scale bar, position\nfrom an ensemble registration, resident-reported control points, sheet-to-sheet image\nalignment and a bundle adjustment over all seven.\n\nThat makes this the only public georeferenced copy of the model, and a dataset is more\nuseful to more people than an argument about it. Everything here loads directly in QGIS:\na PNG carrying the depth bands, a world file placing it, and a .prj naming the CRS.\n\nOutput: docs/data/flood2012/\n\nUsage:  python3 scripts/export_flood.py\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport os\nimport shutil\nimport sys\n\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\nfrom common import DERIVED, ROOT, log, read_json\n\nSRC = os.path.join(DERIVED, \"floodmaps\")\nDEST = os.path.join(ROOT, \"docs\", \"data\", \"flood2012\")\nWGS84_PRJ = ('GEOGCS[\"WGS 84\",DATUM[\"WGS_1984\",SPHEROID[\"WGS 84\",6378137,298.257223563]],'\n             'PRIMEM[\"Greenwich\",0],UNIT[\"degree\",0.0174532925199433]]')\n\nBANDS = [(1, \"0.05-0.1 m\"), (2, \"0.1-0.2 m\"), (3, \"0.2-0.5 m\"),\n         (4, \"0.5-1 m\"), (5, \"1-2 m\"), (6, \"over 2 m\")]\n\n\ndef main():\n    os.makedirs(DEST, exist_ok=True)\n    geo = read_json(os.path.join(SRC, \"_georef.json\"))\n    sheets = read_json(os.path.join(SRC, \"_sheets.json\"))\n    align = None\n    p = os.path.join(DERIVED, \"floodalign.json\")\n    if os.path.exists(p):\n        align = read_json(p)\n\n    manifest = {\n        \"name\": \"Copenhagen 2012 cloudburst flood model, georeferenced\",\n        \"what\": (\"Modelled inundation depth for a 100-year rainfall event on the 2010 \"\n                 \"city, from Københavns Kommune's Skybrudsplan. Recovered from the \"\n                 \"seven published PDF sheets, whose geospatial metadata had been \"\n                 \"stripped, and placed back on the map.\"),\n        \"crs\": \"EPSG:4326\",\n        \"pixel_values\": {str(i): lab for i, lab in BANDS},\n        \"pixel_value_0\": \"no modelled flooding, or outside the sheet\",\n        \"provenance\": [\n            \"Depth bands read by exact match against the printed legend palette.\",\n            \"Scale from each sheet's own scale bar, via the PDF text layer.\",\n            \"Position: an ensemble water cross-correlation for four sheets; control \"\n            \"points located on a web map by a resident for three; then sheet-to-sheet \"\n            \"image registration on masked gradient images and a bundle adjustment over \"\n            \"all seven together.\",\n        ],\n        \"accuracy\": (\"Mutually consistent to 23 m RMS across overlapping sheets, tied to \"\n                     \"the ground by control points with standard errors of 58-91 m. \"\n                     \"Nørrebro is the exception: no overlapping pair produced a usable \"\n                     \"correlation peak and it has no control points, so it keeps its \"\n                     \"original automatic position and is not known to be consistent \"\n                     \"with the rest.\"),\n        \"caveats\": [\n            \"The model is a 2012 calculation of a 2010 scenario. Nordhavn, most of \"\n            \"Ørestad, Sluseholmen and Teglholmen, and much of Refshaleøen have been \"\n            \"built since and are not in it - 344 ha of today's impervious surface, 8.1% \"\n            \"of the city, lies outside every sheet.\",\n            \"The sheets paint depth over lakes and the harbour as well as over land. \"\n            \"Water standing on water is not modelled flooding; filter it against a water \"\n            \"layer if that matters to you.\",\n            \"This is a recovery of a published figure, not a hydraulic model run. Errors \"\n            \"in the recovery are ours, not the city's.\",\n        ],\n        \"licence\": (\"The underlying model is Københavns Kommune's, published as open \"\n                    \"data via opendata.dk. This georeferencing is offered on the same \"\n                    \"terms. Attribute both.\"),\n        \"sheets\": {},\n    }\n\n    for sheet, g in sorted(geo.items()):\n        b = g.get(\"bounds_wgs84\")\n        if not b:\n            continue\n        src_png = os.path.join(SRC, f\"{sheet}.depth.png\")\n        if not os.path.exists(src_png):\n            log(f\"  {sheet}: no depth raster, skipped\")\n            continue\n        shutil.copyfile(src_png, os.path.join(DEST, f\"{sheet}.png\"))\n\n        from PIL import Image\n        w, h = Image.open(src_png).size\n        # world file: pixel sizes and the CENTRE of the top-left pixel\n        px = (b[\"east\"] - b[\"west\"]) / w\n        py = (b[\"north\"] - b[\"south\"]) / h\n        with open(os.path.join(DEST, f\"{sheet}.pgw\"), \"w\") as f:\n            f.write(f\"{px:.12f}\\n0.0\\n0.0\\n{-py:.12f}\\n\"\n                    f\"{b['west'] + px/2:.10f}\\n{b['north'] - py/2:.10f}\\n\")\n        with open(os.path.join(DEST, f\"{sheet}.prj\"), \"w\") as f:\n            f.write(WGS84_PRJ)\n\n        manifest[\"sheets\"][sheet] = {\n            \"raster\": f\"{sheet}.png\",\n            \"world_file\": f\"{sheet}.pgw\",\n            \"size_px\": [w, h],\n            \"bounds_wgs84\": b,\n            \"m_per_px\": g.get(\"m_per_px_from_scalebar\"),\n            \"method\": g.get(\"method\"),\n            \"standard_error_m\": g.get(\"standard_error_m\"),\n            \"bundle_applied\": bool((g.get(\"bundle\") or {}).get(\"applied\")),\n        }\n        log(f\"  {sheet:16} {w}x{h} px  ->  png + pgw + prj\")\n\n    if align:\n        manifest[\"bundle_adjustment\"] = {\n            \"pair_rms_m\": align.get(\"pair_rms_m\"),\n            \"pairs_used\": len(align.get(\"pairs\") or []),\n            \"unconstrained\": align.get(\"unconstrained\"),\n        }\n    for extra in (\"floodgap.json\",):\n        s = os.path.join(DERIVED, extra)\n        if os.path.exists(s):\n            shutil.copyfile(s, os.path.join(DEST, extra))\n\n    with open(os.path.join(DEST, \"manifest.json\"), \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n        json.dump(manifest, f, ensure_ascii=False, indent=1)\n\n    readme = f\"\"\"# Copenhagen 2012 cloudburst flood model, georeferenced\n\n{manifest['what']}\n\n## How to load it\n\nOpen any `<sheet>.png` in QGIS. The matching `.pgw` world file and `.prj` sit beside it,\nso it lands in the right place in EPSG:4326 with no further steps.\n\nPixel values are depth bands, not metres:\n\n| value | depth |\n|---|---|\n{chr(10).join(f\"| {i} | {lab} |\" for i, lab in BANDS)}\n| 0 | no modelled flooding, or outside the sheet |\n\n## Where it came from\n\n{chr(10).join('- ' + p for p in manifest['provenance'])}\n\n## How accurate it is\n\n{manifest['accuracy']}\n\n## What to be careful of\n\n{chr(10).join('- ' + c for c in manifest['caveats'])}\n\n## Licence\n\n{manifest['licence']}\n\nMethod, code and the arguments built on it: https://jjokulian.github.io/copenhagen-waterways/\n\"\"\"\n    with open(os.path.join(DEST, \"README.md\"), \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n        f.write(readme)\n    total = sum(os.path.getsize(os.path.join(DEST, f)) for f in os.listdir(DEST))\n    log(f\"\\nwrote {len(manifest['sheets'])} sheets to docs/data/flood2012/ \"\n        f\"({total/1e6:.1f} MB)\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main())\n","numLines":175,"startLine":1,"totalLines":175}},"displayPath":"scripts/export_flood.py"},"type":"attachment","uuid":"d7da7302-8662-461b-9d07-0557ac62e971","timestamp":"2026-09-08T01:56:52.214Z","rendered":[{"content":"<system-reminder>\nCalled the Read tool with the following input: {\"file_path\":\"/home/user/projects/copenhagen-waterways/scripts/export_flood.py\"}\n</system-reminder>"},{"content":"<system-reminder>\nResult of calling the Read tool:\n1\t#!/usr/bin/env python3\n2\t\"\"\"Publish the recovered flood model as a dataset other people can load.\n3\t\n4\tKøbenhavns Kommune published its 2012 cloudburst model as seven PDF sheets with the\n5\tgeospatial metadata removed - no /Measure, no /GPTS, no /Viewport.
